# Lex integration: user-supplied Track D implementation

Adapted the user-authorized implementation from `C:/Users/aryan/Downloads/ar/aryan/track-d`. Its C++ source and Dockerfile were already present as uncommitted changes; verified source SHA-256 `d518968af3432d6391ea6452cf956f499018ab7d5bb171a61e82cf5fe90ee990` matches the supplied folder exactly. The imported historical evidence below belongs to its source project; it must not be mistaken for Lex board results. The user's reported 72 points is not yet a measurement of this integration.

Replaces the tracked Rust solver with the supplied C++ fused-construction solver: six-byte parse bins, packed adjacency rows, exact degree-two bypassing, BFS locality ordering, reverse rows, paged distance labels and forward-probe/bidirectional bucket searches. Metadata remains Team Lex, corrected to C++, zero runtime dependencies. Preserved supplied pinned offline multi-stage PGO build, using seed 17 solely for training; the generator matches the organizer's file after newline normalization. Its imported CRLF endings are normalized to the exact published LF bytes so the harness recognizes the unmodified organizer generator. No generated training data or profiles enter the runtime image.

Local verification: reproduced instrumentation, trained on a generated full seed-17 graph, and consumed the profile with missing-profile warnings treated as errors. Exact stored tiny/sample/full outputs at 1/4/12 workers; 70 independently generated differential fixtures at all three worker counts, including zero/max-u32 weights, duplicate/parallel edges, self-loops, disconnected vertices, 350-node chains/cycles, and 64-bit distances. AddressSanitizer repeated the same suite successfully. The official Dockerfile allowlist check passes. Docker daemon is unavailable here, so a container build/run is not claimed.

Fifteen randomized paired full-input comparisons, affinity CPUs 0-11, every output checked: previous Rust median 147.884 ms versus imported PGO C++ 86.075 ms (41.8% lower). Externally sampled anonymous peak RSS medians 65,868 versus 62,612 KiB. WSL i7-12700H/GCC 15.2 measurements are not official wall time or cgroup peaks. Prior Rust official best is recorded separately by the board. Official evaluation of this integration is pending; 72+ is not yet confirmed.

Only Track D source/build/metadata/documentation are included in this integration. Previous Rust source remains recoverable from Git history.


First official integration result: `run-2a439d8d4f55`, commit `959a8b6`: all gates pass; T=0.115908 s, M=78,999,552 bytes, S=792, D=1, K=26.274667, **70.976861 points**. Previous Rust best 68.584197. The 72-point target remains unmet. The scoring host commits noticeably more memory than the WSL anonymous-RSS sample. Following the earlier Track A/B/C evidence about transparent huge pages, the retained follow-up adds process-wide PR_SET_THP_DISABLE to prevent partially occupied dense graph buffers from committing whole 2 MiB pages. This is a hypothesis to be measured officially, not a claimed memory improvement. Source no longer matches the supplied file byte-for-byte because of this explicit adaptation.


Integration correction from official evidence: `run-15921d87a024`, commit `8e6e259`, all gates pass; T=0.105029 s, M=69,357,568 bytes, S=1192, D=1, K=26.644821, 71.280640 points. Unlike the original supplied generator bytes (S=792, C++ only), the locally normalized LF copy is counted as Python by the scoring host. The local organizer checkout's byte representation therefore does not match the host's published-copy exemption. Restored the exact original supplied generator bytes to recover the demonstrated S=792 treatment; the earlier assertion that LF normalization would improve recognition was wrong. The generator's semantics and training data are unchanged. Keep process-wide huge-page disabling for its pending evaluation.


## Confirmed 72+ Lex result

Official `run-fb928e7f1666`, implementation `09df66ac6905ceeaab758d73423d747767b5697a`, completed 2026-11-01 07:30:00 UTC: **72.612617 points**, T=0.105865 s, M=67,862,528 bytes, S=794, D=1, K=28.330376. All check/build/correctness/timeout/sandbox gates passed. This meets the user's 72-point Track D target, improving the previous Rust best of 68.584197 by 4.028420 points. The build took 31.810 s with organizer training-stage cache available; earlier cold PGO builds took 133-162 s.

Final code is the supplied solver plus process-wide huge-page disabling; original pinned PGO build remains. The training generator is byte-identical to the organizer's Git blob (SHA-256 `bbf37741159004149c5896265a98ae00b90e90a05ad48c2912ecf8bdce126d65`). The organizer working-tree file had LF normalization, which caused the mistaken intermediate generator edit; direct Git-blob comparison confirms the restored file. The official final S=794/C++-only result confirms published-copy recognition. Do not repeat that working-tree-byte comparison without checking Git blob bytes.

Additional checks: full seed-17 output agrees with the prior Rust solver; fewer construction workers were slower locally and were not retained; CPU-native compilation showed only a small gain and was not retained. A/B/C were not changed. No required work remains for the requested 72+ adaptation.

# Track D evidence

- **Nov 1 query-source-seeded fused BFS, NOT PROMOTED:** [13-pair exact full-seed tournament](https://github.com/insightlabs38-pixel/sd-td/actions/runs/41278025635) tested growing regions from known query sources before sweeping raw IDs. Four-worker wall median baseline/candidate 0.247222/0.248656 s (2/13 wins); six-worker 0.266461/0.266314 s (8/13); twelve-worker 0.271510/0.272359 s (7/13). All 88 differential cases and 78 official full-seed runs matched the oracle. No consistent benefit; champion unchanged. Experiment head `aa796e41`.

- **Nov 1 compact three-slot graph rows, NOT PROMOTED:** [11-pair exact full-seed tournament](https://github.com/insightlabs38-pixel/sd-td/actions/runs/41278238712) compared 12-byte per-vertex rows against 16-byte four-slot rows, with correctly adjusted spill layout in forward, reverse and raw graphs. Four-worker wall 0.247959/0.275895 s (1/11 wins), six-worker 0.265060/0.294910 s (0/11), twelve-worker 0.270332/0.292216 s (1/11). Peak RSS fell only 1,792/1,920/1,920 KiB; insufficient to offset 8-11% slower elapsed. All 88 differential cases and 66 official full-seed runs matched the oracle. Champion unchanged. Experiment head `81e31178`.

- **Nov 1 fused reverse-owner / probe tournament, NOT PROMOTED:** [successful CI](https://github.com/insightlabs38-pixel/sd-td/actions/runs/41274089238) qualified seven variants in 17 matched rounds at 4/6/12 workers (357 exact-output full-seed runs), plus 88 differential fixtures per selected variant, official sample/tiny/full hashes and pinned offline PGO Docker. Forward probe 128 gave medians 0.201805/0.214096/0.214801 s versus the 256-probe baseline 0.202542/0.214857/0.217176 s, but won only 10/17, 8/17, 9/17 paired rounds. Replacing reverse-CSR owner upper_bound with exact integer division regressed at all worker counts (0.202743/0.215624/0.218257 s). No robust cross-worker gain; retain the fused source and PGO Docker. Experimental branch `d/fused-owner-probe-20261101`, head `54788fc7`.

- **Nov 1 region-aligned reverse-CSR ownership, NOT PROMOTED:** [successful CI](https://github.com/insightlabs38-pixel/sd-td/actions/runs/41274502854) compared equal-ID cuts with fused BFS-region cuts over 25 matched full-seed rounds at 4/6/12 workers (150 exact runs), 88 differential cases per variant and official full graph/query hashes. Baseline -> region medians: 4 workers 0.205211 -> 0.204682 s (13/25 wins); 6 workers 0.222373 -> 0.222002 s (16/25); 12 workers 0.223000 -> 0.225496 s (10/25). Twelve-worker native peak RSS 153404 -> 153208 KiB, negligible. Reject due to inconsistent gains and 12-worker regression. Experimental branch `d/fused-region-partition-20261101`, head `70093226`.

- **Nov 1 fused-construction frontier, CANDIDATE (branch `frontier/d-architecture-fused`):** new solver: SIMD parse into 6-byte bins, 4-slot raw rows, one fused pass for degree-2 contraction + BFS ordering + gathering, atomic-free transpose, branch-free FIFO bidirectional search; all phases use every hardware thread. Local paired medians (full seed 1, exact SHA) versus board champion source `be15ac2e`: 4 workers 238.8 -> 201.1 ms with PGO (-16%), 12 workers 256.5 -> 213.8 ms (-17%); cgroup peak at 12 workers with THP=always 80 -> 62 MB; S ~855 -> ~775. Passed 88 differential and 60 stress cases, official hashes, ASan/UBSan/TSan, and CI [41266520621](https://github.com/insightlabs38-pixel/sd-td/actions/runs/41266520621) including pinned offline PGO Docker. Receipts and the architecture-family study (rejected ALT/CH by cost model, measured rejections) in `research/d-architecture-fused.md`. Local KVM proxies, not scoring-box K.

- **Nov 1 two-level bucket occupancy summary, NOT PROMOTED:** [successful exactness/Docker and 17-pass matched tournament](https://github.com/insightlabs38-pixel/sd-td/actions/runs/41266399013) tested a 32-bit word-occupancy summary over the 2048-bucket Dijkstra queue. Four-worker baseline/candidate medians 0.243134/0.243452 s (4/17 candidate wins); six-worker 0.246067/0.246793 s (8/17); twelve-worker 0.258367/0.256237 s (11/17). All 88 differential fixtures, official tiny/sample/full hashes, 102 matched full-seed executions and pinned offline PGO Docker at 1/4/12 workers passed. The summary adds per-push and per-drain bookkeeping without consistent end-to-end gain, so its extra source lines do not earn a K promotion. Experimental branch `d/two-level-bucket-bitmap-20261101`, head `56b74645`; main source `e63d989d` and PGO Dockerfile `66fef24c` retained. GitHub-hosted runner, not scoring-box evidence.

- **Nov 1 distance-page prefetch depths 1/2/3/4/8, NOT PROMOTED:** [21-pass depths 4/8](https://github.com/insightlabs38-pixel/sd-td/actions/runs/41261642136) and [21-pass depths 1/2/3](https://github.com/insightlabs38-pixel/sd-td/actions/runs/41262019806) passed 88 differential fixtures for each challenger, official tiny/sample/full exact hashes and pinned offline PGO Docker for default code. At 4 workers, 1/2 lookahead regressed (baseline 0.219304 s, candidates 0.224962/0.224828 s); at 6 workers depth 2 had only a small median gain (0.223885 -> 0.222962 s, 12/21 paired wins), but at 12 workers regressed (0.226504 -> 0.231165 s, 6/21 wins). Depth 4 improved 12-worker median 0.221183 -> 0.219391 s in the first run but won only 12/21; depths 4/8 lacked consistent gains at 4/6/12. No stable whole-run speedup across worker counts; avoid added branches and distance-page prefetch overhead. Experimental branch `d/distance-page-prefetch-20261101`, head `afd439f8`; retain source `e63d989d` and PGO Dockerfile `66fef24c`. GitHub-runner native comparisons, not official six-core scoring.

- **Nov 1 adjacency-data prefetch, PROMOTED:** [21-pass native tournament](https://github.com/insightlabs38-pixel/sd-td/actions/runs/41257386264) and [25-pass GCC15 PGO tournament](https://github.com/insightlabs38-pixel/sd-td/actions/runs/41257609838) tested next-one versus next-two adjacency lookahead after CSR-offset prefetch. - **Oct 31 contraction survivor-retention experiment: NOT PROMOTED.** The existing BFS construction already discovers shortcut heads reached from surviving seeds, including branch and 64-hop endpoints; explicitly retaining all such heads is unnecessary for exactness. Both source variants passed 88 differential cases; the candidate also passed 60 lattice + 60 contraction-shape stress seeds, official full seed-1 hash, sample holdouts 3/7 and pinned offline Docker. [Matched CI](https://github.com/insightlabs38-pixel/sd-td/actions/runs/41169700209): 15 interleaved rounds at 4 workers, baseline/candidate 0.216944/0.216294 s; at 12 workers 0.237365/0.236199 s; identical reported peak RSS per thread count (69,336/72,808 KiB). With six extra nonblank C++ lines, estimated net K gain is at most about 0.1% on this runner, below promotion confidence. Champion source remains unchanged. Five adversarial regression fixtures were retained in tests.

- **Candidate `frontier/d-claude-architecture-5`: exact degree-2 contraction, in place.** Vertices with out-degree <= 2 that are not query endpoints are bypassed while exactly one way leads on (dead ends dropped, branching vertices stay survivors, 64-step cap, exact 64-bit shortcut weights). The pass rewrites raw edges in place from a snapshot table of the bypassable vertices' edge words, so there is no second edge array; the escape table is merged afterwards. Base: PR 2 champion (`0c455ef` source).
- **What made contraction profitable (vs the neutral `-4` experiment):** the pass fell from 84-91 ms to 58.7 ms CPU at one thread (18.4 ms wall at four) by dropping the 20 MB `res` copy and its stores (in place), reading walk starts from a flat edge loop with branchless start recording and deferred root recovery, and using larger blocks. Measured attribution: the old scan was dominated by per-vertex loop-exit mispredicts and by 40-byte walk records written for every edge; the walks themselves were only 37 ms.
- **Paired local result (4 cores, 41 interleaved rounds vs PR 2):** median 0.245 vs 0.271 s (-9.6%), min 0.216 vs 0.245 s; fresh-cgroup peak at 12 threads with THP=always 74.8 vs 81.5 MB (-8%); formatted lines 855 vs 721. Local K estimate x1.052 (T) x1.017 (M) x0.975 (S) = about +4.3%. Not scoring-box data; the 4-core factors may not transfer.
- **Qualification:** 83 differential cases and 120-seed lattice plus contraction-shape stress at 1/4/12 workers; ASan/UBSan and TSan clean on both suites and sample data at 1/4/12 workers; 30 full-size and 150 sample repeat runs at 1/4/12 workers with zero mismatches; tiny/sample seed 2 byte-identical to the Python reference; sample 1 and full seed 1 SHAs match (full also at 12 threads). Pinned Docker run at 1/4/12 workers in CI.
- **Measured and not adopted this round:** (1) fusing the bypass flags and edge snapshot into the raw CSR build: no time gain and the 12-thread peak rose 74.8 to 91 MB because the 14 MB snapshot then coexists with the bins; (2) SSSE3/SSE4.1 parsing: a 16-byte load plus one shuffle converts `u` and `v` together and cuts parse CPU 116 to 98.5 ms (-15%) once the parse lambda carries the target attribute so it inlines, but a SIMD `w` on top is slower (105 ms, serialized behind the separator search); the pair-only variant is worth about 2% of T for +25 lines, a net of about +0.5% K.
- **Rollback:** PR 2 champion (K=23.855961).

Previous history follows.


- **Board result for the donation candidate (PR 3): rejected.** Luna's controlled A/B scored K=22.276 (T=0.148357 s, M=98.5 MB, S=779) against K=23.856 (T=0.132055 s, M=98.4 MB, S=728) for PR 2. The 12-thread balance model predicted a tie; it missed the cost of the idle-thread yield/spin loop (an SMT sibling's resources) and the mutex-protected donation queue. Lesson: the board's T tracks total CPU work divided by about 7.8 effective cores, not critical-path balance, so changes must be judged by CPU work.
- **Exact degree-2 contraction (branch `frontier/d-claude-architecture-4`, source blob `git hash-object track-d/d_traverse.cpp`): neutral, not proposed.** Vertices with out-degree <= 2 that are not query endpoints are bypassed by a unique-continuation walk (dead ends dropped, branching vertices stay survivors, 64-step cap forces a survivor); shortcut weights are exact 64-bit. Offline it removes 31% of vertices and 22% of edges, shortens solve CPU by 27-33%, and shrinks reverse/grow/gather. The price is a resolve pass over all 5M edges (110-125 ms CPU: 36 baseline plus 76 for walks) that nearly cancels the gain: total CPU -3% at 1 thread and flat at 4. Paired 4-core timing median -6.5% (0.288 vs 0.308 s over 41 rounds), but +131 formatted lines (852 vs 721; S about -2.4% K) and +3 MB (about -0.7% K), so the net K estimate is about zero. It would pay if the resolve pass cost under about 60 ms CPU.
- **Qualification of that branch:** 83 differential cases and 80+80-seed lattice and contraction-shape stress (long chains past the cap, one-way rings, diamonds, hubs, heavy edges) at 1/4/12 workers; ASan/UBSan and TSan clean; 144 repeat runs at 1/4/12 workers with zero mismatches; tiny/sample seed 2 byte-identical to the Python reference; sample 1 and full seed 1 SHAs match.
- **Dead ends measured this round:** 32-bit labels (slower, more instructions), forward probe length (identical instruction counts), push pruning by the other side's bound, prefetch of permutation entries, bucket-array size, query scheduling policies (ceiling about 2% under a 6-core SMT model), huge-page hints, and a private visited filter plus claim prefetch in the BFS (within noise).

Previous history follows.


- **Promoted frontier2 (Oct 31, source blob `8da532c2b6c4ab112eb7e795b2d827728498ba60`):** [independent matched CI](https://github.com/insightlabs38-pixel/sd-td/actions/runs/41156102977) passed 83 differential fixtures, 120 lattice stress seeds, nine interleaved official full-seed-1 A/B runs with exact output SHA on all 18 runs, and official full seed 2 byte-for-byte against the Python oracle (SHA `23d60c06b8e2c6086f765e4a9a3640306c3bf8199149fd39b20e1ef56b3b8a4b`). GitHub-hosted runner median full-process wall **0.42 -> 0.33 s (21.4% lower)**, peak RSS **87,936 -> 69,120 KiB (21.4% lower)**, user CPU **1.42 -> 1.10 s**. Rough nonblank/noncomment C++ lines **512 -> 529**; T/M-only proxy K ratio **1.184**, with approximate source penalty ~0.5% and no dependency change. Not official scoring-box K. Source-identical candidate passed offline Docker checks in [CI](https://github.com/insightlabs38-pixel/sd-td/actions/runs/41154994513).

- **Promoted frontier champion (Oct 31, source SHA `576af9c23bbbdfa16838a4737342ab2063176615`):** parallel bucketed parse and CSR build, BFS-region renumbering for locality, 4-byte packed edges with exact escape table, exact-key bucket queue, paged per-worker labels, queries scheduled by source id. Local 4-core VM, full seed 1, interleaved A/B against the previous champion: median wall **0.33 s vs 1.10 s (3.3x)**, peak RSS **91 MB vs 227 MB**, exact output SHA `a23dbdf0...` every run. Not scoring-box data; scoring-box thread scaling remains unverified.
- **Independent Chat qualification (Oct 31):** [successful matched CI](https://github.com/insightlabs38-pixel/sd-td/actions/runs/41151166030), branch `d/frontier-independent-qualification`, is source-identical to promoted `576af9c` (only the workflow differs). Seven interleaved full-seed-1 passes, all exact SHA: previous champion median wall **0.96 s**, frontier **0.29 s** (**3.31x faster**); median peak RSS **227,320 to 87,944 KiB** (**61.3% less**); median user CPU **2.77 to 0.96 s**. Full seed 2 matched the official Python reference byte-for-byte (output SHA-256 `23d60c06b8e2c6086f765e4a9a3640306c3bf8199149fd39b20e1ef56b3b8a4b`); 120 additional lattice stress seeds, 83 differential fixtures, official tiny/sample/full seed-1 and offline Docker sample/full checks all passed. Rough nonblank noncomment C++ lines grew 292 to 512; estimated combined T/M/S K ratio about **2.0x** under published exponents, not an official board or scoring-box result.
- **Thread/probe sweep (Oct 31):** [successful CI](https://github.com/insightlabs38-pixel/sd-td/actions/runs/41151731082) ran 48 exact-output-hash-checked full-seed-1 executions (three passes each across 16 configurations). On this GitHub runner, median wall/RSS were 4 threads **0.38 s / 87,936 KiB**, 6 threads **0.41 s / 89,104 KiB**, 12 threads **0.45 s / 99,960 KiB**. With four threads, probes 64, 128, 256 and 512 all rounded to 0.38 s; keep 256. GH-runner results do not establish the optimum on the official 6-P-core/12-thread box, so the existing default is unchanged pending scored measurement.
- **Gates:** 83 differential cases x 3 thread counts x 6 probe limits; tiny seed 2 and sample seed 2 byte-identical to the Python reference; sample seed 1 and full seed 1 SHA match the published values; ASan/UBSan clean; pinned Docker build and run match ([CI run](https://github.com/insightlabs38-pixel/sd-td/actions/runs/41148327539)).
- **Measured by phase (4 threads, ms):** parse+bin 30, raw CSR 15, BFS regions 35, assemble 8, gather 22, reverse 23, queries ~150. Single-thread query CPU ~0.6 s for 7.4M settles.
- **Findings:** random relabeling made every label access a cache miss; BFS renumbering alone cut query CPU 2.4x. A shared claim bitmap was fine, but adjacent per-region counters false-shared and cost 2x until moved to locals. Atomic CSR scatter cost 85 ms vs 15 ms for bucketed no-atomic build. Packed/Dial-style queues helped only once the bucket array was lean. Transparent huge pages gave a small median gain but a bad tail on this VM, so they were removed. A forward probe of 256 settles is required: pure bidirectional search floods the 1.7M-node main component for island-source queries.

Previous champion history follows.

s / 264 MB across varying host contention. Radix won repeated matched comparisons; 16-bit stamps and alternative direction policies were not promoted.
- **Official scored board:** no Track D PERF run ID yet; the integrator owns official submission and scored performance verification.

- **Promoted in-place radix redistribution:** official full seed-1 matched A/B benchmark on the same GitHub-hosted runner: baseline wall seconds 1.53/1.55/1.50/1.53/1.54 (median 1.53); promoted 1.46/1.38/1.42/1.34/1.45 (median 1.42), **7.2% lower median wall time**, with exact output hash every run. Peak RSS approximately 263,716 KiB baseline vs 265,504 KiB promoted (+0.7%); CPU user median 4.30 s vs 3.94 s. All 83 differential cases and official tiny/sample/full gates, native and Docker full outputs passed in [successful matched CI](https://github.com/insightlabs38-pixel/sd-td/actions/runs/41093289804). Not official scoring-box data.

- **Promoted forward-probe continuation (Oct 30):** preserve the first 256 settled forward Dijkstra states when transitioning to bidirectional search, rather than restarting. Matched full seed-1 CI (five A/B/C interleaved passes): baseline 1.59/1.56/1.46/1.47/1.43 s (median 1.47 s); reuse 1.43/1.51/1.44/1.39/1.38 s (median 1.43 s), approximately 2.7% faster, comparable 265 MiB RSS; 83 differential cases with 5 probe limits, official seed-1 exact SHA and Docker checks pass. Source grew 294 to 306 nonblank noncomment lines, yielding an estimated 0.8% K gain under the published exponents, not an official score. [Qualified CI](https://github.com/insightlabs38-pixel/sd-td/actions/runs/41101426555).

- **Promoted compact 16-bit epochs + early parser scratch release (Oct 30):** matched seven-pass full seed-1 comparison (same GitHub runner, exact SHA each pass) against preceding champion: median wall **0.96 to 0.91 s** (5.2% lower), median RSS **265,508 to 238,328 KiB** (10.2% lower), median user CPU **2.87 to 2.70 s**. Exact official full/sample/tiny and Docker gates pass; 83 differential fixtures at 5 probe limits plus a 66,000-query epoch-wrap regression pass. Source nonblank/noncomment lines 306 to 308; estimated **4.85% K improvement** under published weights, not official scoring-box K. [Successful matched tournament CI](https://github.com/insightlabs38-pixel/sd-td/actions/runs/41109562144). The two initial standalone experiment workflows failed at a shallow-checkout benchmark `git show`, not at solver correctness; the final tournament corrected checkout depth and completed.

- **Compact u32 distances + sparse exact u64 overflow, not promoted:** [candidate](https://github.com/insightlabs38-pixel/sd-td/tree/d/compact-distance-overflow) passed 83 differential cases, official full seed-1 SHA and Docker. Seven matched passes versus the preceding u32-stamp baseline gave median wall **1.06 vs 1.09 s** (candidate slower 2.8%), RSS **265,464 vs 261,464 KiB** (candidate only 1.5% lower), source 306 vs 332 nonblank/noncomment lines. Estimated K **2.3% worse**, so the experiment remains off main. The limited RSS reduction despite smaller distance arrays indicates a different phase (likely parsing) dominates peak high-water; this is an inference, not a measured allocation profile. [CI](https://github.com/insightlabs38-pixel/sd-td/actions/runs/41110030732).

- **Promoted 8-bit generation stamps (Oct 30):** exact 8-bit epoch wrap resets both forward/reverse stamp arrays; the existing 66,000-query rollover test passed along with 83 differential cases at 5 probe limits, official full seed-1 hash and Docker. Seven matched full seed-1 passes against the 16-bit champion: median wall **1.01 to 0.97 s** (4.0% faster), median RSS **238,328 to 227,320 KiB** (4.6% lower), median user CPU **2.96 to 2.78 s**; no source-line growth. Estimated K gain about **3.0%** under frozen scoring weights; GitHub-hosted runner, not scoring box. [Successful CI](https://github.com/insightlabs38-pixel/sd-td/actions/runs/41110514088).


## Highest-K Track D sprint

Target: exceed the live Track D leader's K, not only improve runtime. Initial live snapshot: leader K=29.335266; Lex K=28.446079 (72.701120 points, same preceding solver). Research: RoutingKit contraction-hierarchy documentation and the original customizable-contraction-hierarchies paper confirm that preprocessing cost must be paid; no heavyweight routing index or third-party dependency was introduced. Experiments are preserved outside the submission in /home/aryan/lex-d-top.

Retained challenger: release discovery-order buffers once the raw-to-final ID map has been constructed; release the claim bitmap after growth; reduce contraction walk batches from 32 to 8; replace iterative bit-width calculation with the equivalent leading-zero intrinsic on valid input bounds; reuse Rows::at in reverse construction rather than duplicate its spill-address logic. Build both PGO stages with CPU-native code generation and exceptions/RTTI/frame pointers disabled. These flags add no source/dependency penalty. PGO training remains on organizer-generated seed 17.

Thirty-seven randomized, alternating full-input comparisons with exact output checks: baseline PGO median 81.195 ms; retained combination 77.438 ms (4.6% lower). Native cleanup alone 79.366 ms, smaller batches alone 77.786 ms; combined default flags 77.772 ms, forced no-unroll 78.252 ms. Earlier 31-run comparison likewise favored the native cleanup. These are WSL i7-12700H/GCC15 timings, not official evidence of first place.

Rejected: page-aligned per-bucket allocation/reclamation (too many syscalls), grouped temporary-bin reclamation (lower local RSS but insufficient complete-K gain), streamed output-buffer reclamation, narrower distance pages, byte-sized degrees with exact overflow, compact 32-bit labels with exact u64 overflow (slower), alternate atomic claim policies, a vector-backed bucket queue, sequential contraction, and larger batch sizes. All timed variants were compared with full exact bytes before performance conclusions. Retained candidate passes stored tiny/sample/full and 70 independent differential fixtures at 1/4/12 workers. Sanitizer verification and official evaluation are recorded as they complete. No A/B/C changes are part of this sprint.

AddressSanitizer also passed the same stored datasets and all 70 differential fixtures at 1/4/12 workers.


### Official sprint result and smaller queue-ring follow-up

`run-67d18200b211`, implementation `3632eb4`: all gates pass; T=0.100928 s, M=67,416,064 bytes, S=789, D=1, K=29.080881, **73.180378 points**. New Lex best, but below the observed first-place K=29.335266.

Follow-up changes only the near-queue ring from 2048 to 128 buckets. Bucket width remains 128; larger jumps still use the exact 64-bit priority queue and are promoted into the ring by the existing lower-bound logic. No weight or distance is truncated. Twenty-five randomized full-output-checked memory-sampled runs: current solver median 89.507 ms, 128-bucket ring 86.419 ms (3.4% lower), 256/512/1024 rings 89.556/89.814/91.435 ms. Sampled anonymous peaks are unchanged because construction remains the peak. Grouped bin release with the current solver saved memory but regressed time and remains excluded. ### Queue result; combined memory challenger

`run-de83a95c5283`, commit `5182132`: all gates pass, T=0.101526 s, M=67,473,408 bytes, S=789, D=1, K=28.990180, 73.112545 points. It did not improve the prior best. A fresh complete board read found the newer leader SweetDreams at K=29.562529; first place remains unmet.

Combined challenger partitions each parser worker's temporary edge arena into independent groups of sixteen tail buckets. One fill worker owns every bucket of a group and releases its arena pages only after both degree counting and edge placement have finished for that group. The discarded prefix cannot overlap any still-live group's page-aligned stride. This batches reclamation into group-sized ranges instead of the rejected per-page syscall design. The discovery-order and claim buffers remain released after their final reads. Mapped buffers now use standard unique_ptr ownership with a munmap deleter rather than handwritten move/destruction logic. The 128-bucket queue is retained within this separately evaluated combination.

Twenty-nine randomized full-output-checked memory-sampled comparisons: previous best-code proxy 77.560 ms / 62,732 KiB peak anonymous RSS; grouped ownership challenger 78.434 ms / 57,772 KiB. Runtime +1.1%, sampled memory -7.9%; these are local proxy axes, not an official K result. Explicit heap trimming was slower and excluded. Challenger passes five stored scale/worker checks through the existing verification suite (tiny/sample/full at 1/4/12), 70 independent randomized differential fixtures at 1/4/12, AddressSanitizer on that suite, and an additional shuffled 70,004-vertex cross-group cycle with zero/max-u32 weights, duplicate/self edges, isolated queries and large exact u64 distances at 1/4/12 in both optimized and sanitized builds. No first-place claim before official scoring.


### Grouped-arena official result and compact-block replacement

`run-1c70668f24a3`, implementation `3e07fe7`: all gates pass; T=0.127713 s, M=61,976,576 bytes, S=798, D=1, K=26.246060, 70.953206 points. Memory improved but the scoring-host runtime regression was too large, so this layout is replaced. It reserved widely separated virtual group strides and advised large ranges; local sampled peak VmPTE was 3,044 KiB versus about 570 KiB for compact allocation.

Replacement assigns each group exclusive 64 KiB fragments from one compact mapping. Chunks within a fragment still hold 256 six-byte entries. A fragment is handed out only when its group cannot fit another chunk; its group records the fragment address and releases it after the group's last read. No fragment belongs to two groups. The arena capacity covers all complete chunks plus one partially occupied fragment per group, using BLOCK - CHUNK*sizeof(Entry) as the conservative payload bound. Reclamation now touches only owned 64 KiB ranges, avoiding the sparse strides. Standard unique_ptr/munmap ownership is retained. Per-array no-huge-page calls are removed because the process already disables THP globally. Near queue has 64 buckets; the exact far heap remains unchanged.

PGO is trained with twelve workers to match runtime. Thirty-seven randomized output-checked full-input comparisons: previous best proxy 77.505 ms / 62,724 KiB anonymous peak, compact arena with four-worker training 77.077 ms / 58,040 KiB, compact arena with twelve-worker training 75.809 ms / 58,040 KiB. Training the old layout at twelve workers alone did not help (77.655 ms), so the result is specific to the combined layout. Ordinary compact-block variant: peak page-table footprint about 568 KiB. These are local measurements, not an official first-place result.

Selected optimized and AddressSanitizer binaries pass stored tiny/sample/full at 1/4/12 workers, all 70 independent differential fixtures at 1/4/12, and the shuffled 70,004-vertex cross-group cycle with exact large-distance oracle at 1/4/12. Source/build remain zero-dependency C++, only Track D changed. Leader last verified at K=29.562529; first place remains pending official evaluation.


### Compact-arena official result; persistent pool and AVX2 challenger

`run-2ba8d19cba95`, implementation `eac4560`: all gates pass; T=0.103861 s, M=61,825,024 bytes, S=804, D=1, K=29.085740, **73.184006 points**. This is the new Lex best, still below the last verified leader K=29.562529.

Next challenger reuses the initial worker set for all phases with C++20 start/completion barriers. Scoped callbacks are published before the start barrier, and cleared only after every callback has returned through the completion barrier. Smaller active worker counts are guarded per worker; the pool's capacity remains the initial count. The main thread drives phases sequentially. Workers and their backing state intentionally have process lifetime, matching this program's explicit exit/PGO-flush model. No scoped callback remains published across a phase boundary.

The structural scanner uses two 32-byte AVX2 comparisons instead of four 16-byte SSE2 comparisons, producing the identical 64-bit delimiter mask. Forward probe limit changes from 256 to 16; bidirectional search and its exact lower-bound termination remain unchanged. The pinned build now uses C++20, still zero external dependencies and twelve-worker PGO training.

Local 37-run randomized full-output comparison: compact baseline 77.232 ms; persistent pool 77.635 ms; pool plus AVX2 75.136 ms. Separate 29-run probe comparison: compact 77.848 ms, pool/AVX2 with probe 256 77.521 ms, probe 16 76.423 ms; probes 32/64/128 77.301/76.598/76.834 ms. Variance limits these as local proxies; no first-place claim is based on them. Rejected: tagged 32-bit labels, speculative 32-bit searches with exact u64 fallback, separate retry phases, standard PMR arenas, batched reclamation, and additional compiler/queue variants; none established a better complete trade-off than the selected candidate.

Selected optimized and AddressSanitizer builds pass stored tiny/sample/full at 1/4/12, all 70 independent differential fixtures at 1/4/12, the shuffled 70,004-vertex large-distance cross-group cycle, empty and single-query files, and an exact 8,589,934,590-distance query. ThreadSanitizer passes stored datasets; its isolated fixture verification is recorded below when complete. Two concurrent test runners initially shared scratch fixture paths, invalidating one comparison; that run is excluded and rerun in a dedicated directory. No Track A/B/C changes.

ThreadSanitizer isolated rerun: stored tiny/sample/full and all 70 independent differential fixtures at 1/4/12 workers pass with halt_on_error enabled.

## Single cyclic barrier follow-up

The persistent pool now reuses one C++20 cyclic barrier for its start and completion phases. Every worker finishes the callback before the completion phase, and the next callback remains protected by the following start phase. Worker capacity and the smaller-query guards remain unchanged. The winning code at f94eb34 remains the rollback baseline.

Thirty-nine randomized interleaved full seed-1 comparisons with exact output checks: winner median 82.181 ms, single barrier 79.709 ms (3.0% lower). A separate 21-pair full seed-17 comparison against independent Rust reference outputs was essentially tied: 72.051 versus 71.942 ms. Local WSL/GCC15 measurements do not establish an official K gain. A single-word queue occupancy specialization was slower and excluded.

Optimized, AddressSanitizer and ThreadSanitizer builds each pass stored tiny/sample/full datasets and all 70 independent differential fixtures at 1/4/12 workers. All three builds also pass empty queries, a single query with idle pool workers, and exact distance 8,589,934,590 at 1/4/12. Isolated sequential scratch directories prevent fixture interference. Only Track D source changes; generator bytes and pinned Docker build remain unchanged. Official evaluation pending.

## 2026-11-02: Reverse scratch reuse and query prefetch candidate

## 2026-11-02: 75-point challenger, unique claims and final-ID packing

Rollback source f3c7439 is first-place CSR. Its documentation-only rerun 789ebdb scored K=30.801549 / 74.428628, T=0.100098 s, M=60,833,792, S=634 (run-5817dbf0cd8d), improving the first 74.373046 result without source changes.

Selected challenger: global atomic fetch-or claims with a read filter replace per-worker membership bitmaps; claims are monotone and each raw vertex has one region owner. Region base prefixes are computed before mapping, allowing final IDs to be written directly. The map stores three bytes per ID when final IDs fit 24 bits, with a four-byte fallback, checked allocation and padded safe loads. Unique ownership permits non-overlapping three-byte writes; all writes complete before remapping reads. Queue ring is 64 rather than 2048. Exhausted narrow probes with no overflow can report proven unreachable directly; finite results at/above UINT32_MAX and any overflow still trigger exact u64 retry. Standard joined worker phases reduce S relative to the persistent pool. AVX2, generator bytes, pinned build and twelve-worker seed-17 PGO are unchanged.

39-way interleaved full-data trial medians: baseline 93.476 ms, 64-ring 91.061, atomic claims 91.816, direct u32 map 93.049, packed map/persistent pool 90.667, selected packed map/joined workers 89.585. Sampled peak anonymous KiB: baseline 56,312; selected 54,320. Canonical source estimate including unchanged build: baseline S=634, selected S=617. These are WSL proxies, not official axes.

Fresh 41-round-per-seed confirmation: seed1 baseline/candidate medians 92.686/93.597 ms, selected wins 26/41 and median paired ratio 0.9820; seed17 93.433/90.518 ms, wins 25/41, ratio 0.9822. Absolute medians and paired ratios differ under scheduling variation; no gain is claimed solely from the initial 4% result. Lower source and sampled memory support an official complete-K trial.

Rejected: construction caps 4/6/8 were slower (101.949/85.902/80.953 vs 77.920 ms baseline); shared retry label/queue caches did not lower the construction peak and were slower; non-PGO compilation was slower; shorter probes, additional prefetch hints and unconditional atomic claims did not establish better complete trade-offs. All timed public full outputs were checked byte-for-byte.

Optimized PGO, ASan/UBSan and TSan pass public tiny/sample/full, 70 existing fixtures, 88 supplied differential cases, 60 u32-boundary cases, 30 lattice plus 30 contraction-shape cases, shuffled 70,004-node cycle, empty/single queries and exact large-u64 paths at 1/4/12 workers. A scratch forced-four-byte-map ASan/UBSan build passes the same suite. All four builds pass 600-node paths totaling UINT32_MAX-1 / UINT32_MAX / UINT32_MAX+1 / 5,990,000,000 and a 70,005-node skewed star with all vertices terminal and heavy edges. Sanitizers halt on error with no findings; program-lifetime leak checks and TSan exit grace sleep are disabled as previously documented.

## 2026-11-02: compact degree counts and label directories

Track D only. Replace per-region u32 cumulative edge ends with byte out-degrees and an ordered exact u32 overflow list (255 and above). This releases three bytes per processed vertex without truncating large degrees. Replace pointer page directories with u32 element offsets, reserving page zero as the immutable blank page. Keep 128 labels per page and the persistent phase pool. A 64-bucket near ring retains the exact far-distance heap. Exhausted narrow searches can report unreachable without an unnecessary u64 pass, while any overflow flag still forces the wide retry.

Local PGO compilation uses the unchanged Docker flags/training seed and checks missing-profile errors. Two independent full inputs, 41 randomized interleaved rounds each: base median 81.596 / 84.747 ms; candidate 80.015 / 83.216 ms; median paired ratios 0.9820 / 0.9803. Sampled RssAnon medians (five runs, KiB): base 56,100; compact128 53,336; compact256 54,416. Local scheduling is noisy: these are directional proxies, not predicted official points. Estimated formatted source increases from 634 to 649 including Dockerfile, so source cost remains part of the tradeoff. Compact256 provided no consistent speed advantage. Joined per-phase threads in compactSpawn measured 97.713 ms versus persistent compact128 97.149 ms in a separate 31-round comparison and are not selected here.

Validation passed separately for optimized static PGO, ASan+UBSan, and TSAN: tiny/sample/full reference bytes; 70 independent differential fixtures at 1/4/12 workers; shuffled 70,004-vertex cross-group cycle; empty/single-query cases; 88 independent oracle cases; 60 integer-boundary cases; 30 randomized lattice/contraction stress cases. Targeted tests passed in all three builds at 1/4/12 workers: 600-node chains spanning u32 sentinel and above; 70,005-node skewed star with every node terminal; out-degrees 254/255/256/511; mixed overflow degrees in one graph. Sanitizers halt on errors; leak checking disabled for intentional process-lifetime mappings; TSAN exit sleep zero avoids shutdown delay without disabling race checks.

## 2026-11-02: combine packed construction with compact search state

First compact persistent-pool official trial run-01fab7cd4dfd, commit075d783: all gates passed; points74.424499, K30.795694, T0.101608 s, M57,634,816 bytes, S649. Memory improved but total score did not exceed the earlier best.

Combined candidate uses the independently validated packed-map/atomic-claim construction and joined phase threads from 4dabb25, plus byte degrees and offset directories above. Final IDs are assigned before building the map; map entries use 3 bytes up to 2^24 vertices and exact 4 bytes above that. Atomic fetch_or gives unique ownership before non-overlapping map writes.
